# OPH reverse-engineering table: model and theorem premises

As of 2026-10-30. Generated from [the navigation map](../tracking/premise_register.json)
by `python tools/build_model_theorem_map.py`. Edit the input, then regenerate.

This map connects the canonical premise identifiers and active model obligations to their current construction evidence. It is navigation coverage, not an extraction of every paper or Lean antecedent and not a proof of discharge. Alternative branches retain their own hypotheses; cross-references do not change claim-registry dependency roles.

**Construct the simplest shared repair process satisfying the declared observational contract.** Architecture, observer services and the complete repair law can supply inputs. Their consequences need not be postulated again. A theorem attachment must identify the same states, instruments, retained records, access rules and scheduling domain; matching component formulas alone is insufficient. Unique selection of an implementation is separate from executing that implementation and testing its predictions.

The live construction is `model/`. Its native C/E instruments determine local exchange and coherence laws, finite stationary preparation, field and event-clock statistics, and complete quantum observation bounds. The finite transfer logarithm, local Liouville generator and modular action on a chosen algebra/reference are distinct constructions. An observer-relative clock is an accessible record process; no preferred universal physical clock is required. Supplied scalar, gauge and matter actions remain conditional benchmarks with their own occupation, temporal and spatial error contracts.

**Completion concerns observations.** The intended effective quantum-field, classical and gravitational descriptions must reproduce compatible joint statistics and intervention responses within controlled regimes. Fields, metrics and manifolds need not be fundamental objects. Native source execution does not by itself establish physical spacetime or identify a laboratory apparatus. Public oscillator comparisons retain their calibration, postselection, coordinate and sampling limitations; numerical agreement, mathematical consistency and code fidelity are separate from physical identification.

The entries distinguish finite executions, analytic bounds, imported theorem hypotheses and remaining physical attachments. Separate actions, preparations, detectors and approximation domains keep separate certificates. Reusing a theorem requires a valid map of its actual hypotheses, not a count of available results or the simultaneous imposition of incompatible branches.

The [current physical bridge](SOURCE_SELECTION.md#current-bridge-to-physical-observations) distinguishes finite observer field/clock results, the remaining effective QFT and spacetime identification, and actual public-data tests. No federation is required for the completed single-carrier calculations. Issue #1077 retains its original q=3 scalar-word deliverable; smaller executed histories do not replace it.

Many entries cite executable model packages that supply finite instances of a premise interface: overlapping-observer and incident-clock quantum correspondences, compiled apparatus words and a compiled clock history, the active event clock and the inverse clock, the native continuum operator and causal placement, record-law identification, finite equilibration and retrospective public-data attachment audits. Such a citation supplies a finite instance on a stated domain. It discharges no canonical premise, changes no disposition and promotes no retrospective comparison to a prospective test.

The exhaustive contract inventory below covers every registered claim and tracked Lean source module. Restricted same-model bindings retain all canonical inputs and require explicit domain maps before composition. Missing model realizations remain visible; coverage and typed compatibility do not certify scientific entailment or physical completion.

The current finite interfaces are recorded in [SAME_PROCESS_INTERFACES.md](completion/SAME_PROCESS_INTERFACES.md) and model/completion/finite_attachment_map.json. They assign all 84 canonical rows to completion owners and distinguish 17 realized finite attachments among 22 reviewed domains from zero canonical physical discharges. Complete source reference recovery, charged response and contact interaction extend their stated finite domains; retrospective mass and ACT/CODATA comparisons add zero eligible physical tests. The larger claim/Lean inventory remains navigation rather than a new proof audit.

These groups are workgroup navigation crosswalks, not claim-consumer edges or changes
to proof status. A group links related construction, branch, downstream or metrology
work; that association does not make every listed premise an antecedent of every
listed theorem. Existing claim-registry edges and `explicit_non_consumer` declarations
remain authoritative. Physical attachments excluded by a theorem are not hidden premises
of that theorem. Group statuses describe the remaining connection, not whether a theorem
is proved; the constructed results are recorded separately below. This schema contains
no proof-discharge edges; the implementation status of each premise is the separate
[discharge table](#what-the-current-implementation-does-with-each-premise).

## What the current implementation does with each premise

The executable construction in this directory is the current implementation of the
specification's dynamical details; the twelve-port carrier is fixed, the details are not.
For each canonical premise the register records whether this implementation derives it
from native records, constructs an instance on declared inputs, proves an obstruction,
takes it as a declared input, or does not address it. The status certifies no physical
premise, and a theorem-library result holds on its premises whatever the status.

| Status | Premises |
| --- | ---: |
| derived from native records | 3 |
| constructed on declared inputs | 5 |
| obstructed | 5 |
| declared input | 17 |
| not addressed | 54 |

## Remaining-input and dependency rule

For a selected scope G, let I(g) denote each group's required inputs on its
declared family, state, observable algebra, clock, regime and quantitative error domain.
The remaining-input set is U(G) = union over g in G of I(g), with genuinely identical
inputs counted once. This is a bookkeeping rule, not a machine-computed logical set:
the prose, ordinary assumptions and imports require semantic review. Active
construction, optional branches, downstream physical attachments and metrology are
separate scopes; unioning them does not turn all of them into theorem antecedents.

A result H implies C discharges an input P only when C establishes P on that same
domain, with the required errors, and H has independent supplied or established
support. Substitution retains any outstanding inputs of H. Circular references,
a change of regulator/state/code, or an imported calibration relabeled as a derivation
do not close an obligation. Establishing a conditional theorem and realizing its
hypotheses in the candidate are distinct tasks. This tool checks neither entailment
nor acyclicity of scientific arguments and changes no existing dependency edges.

## Workgroup crosswalk

For the active path, RE-05–RE-12 form the connected action, inertia, quantum
representation, state, history, refinement and clock cluster. Downstream rows
are not additional prerequisites for the supplied finite scalar theorem.

### RE-20: Parameter selection and P

**Constructed:** Exact conditional identities, normalized-capacity reconstruction and selection boundaries are recorded in the existing ledgers. The finite detector accepts P and N as closure-derived premises, executes the existing P-to-alpha map, and uses its chosen charge assignment and calibrated loop exposure to produce a retained detector bit and P-dependent native feedback. A conditional matter-trace/running/entropy branch is isolated; Thomson, threshold, scale and same-cell identifications retain their premises.

**Remaining input:** The detector is a conditional design under accepted closure inputs; its charge assignment, probe, flux, readout and protocol are specified apparatus. Executing the existing source-only coupling branch does not upgrade its physical endpoint status. The same-current local field uses the full 32-level exterior action and its fifteen-component sector on separate physical CAR modes. Native current rotations, exchange echoes and fermionic swaps supply gauge-linked hopping, with complete four-column factors and an unrestricted-Fock source norm bound. Independent site gauges and a nontrivial plaquette check local background covariance. Constant noncommuting source links have a controlled fixed-band, fixed-particle-number continuum comparison with explicit splitting and accumulated source error; refinement requires improving source accuracy. Both Dirac tastes, both chiralities and every auxiliary complement remain present. This is background-linked vectorlike matter, not quantum gauge dynamics, a chiral measure, a physical vacuum or the observed spectrum. The current interacting CAR field, exact energy currents, CP regional maps, full-fiber thermal controls and native M2 clock attachment share declared local terms with explicitly distinguished finite boundary conditions. The entire source/field/reference/physical-regime composition remains unconstructed.

**Remaining input:** Preserve the actual state, instruments, records, access rules and clock when composing these results. Each action, geometry, initialization and control architecture is declared; physical proper-code load/export, memory and readout accuracy require their own evidence. The two-carrier history is not the twelve-million-record mass campaign, and the free cube is not a full many-carrier C/E service history. Different interactions need new bounds; no free-field continuum or wedge theorem transfers automatically to the interacting gauge/scalar or alternating-path action. A common physical chiral spectrum, responsive metric, renormalized area law, laboratory units and independent cross-scale tests remain unestablished. No full Lean OperationalObserver/federation instantiation or simultaneous realization of incompatible finite hypotheses is claimed. The specified current menu and chosen spin/exterior lift provide a conditional same-object construction. Physical completeness of the accessible current, local gauge dynamics, physical spin/statistics, vacuum, clock/units and an uncalibrated mass-generating law matching observations remain required; the measured spectrum is not derived. The 17-current result excludes the stated exchange law and does not contradict a classification conditional on complete 12-dimensional response. The executable construction realizes calibrated Standard Model charged-matter structure and permits observer-record recovery of its supplied mass parameters. Their source derivation and the complete interacting physical continuum remain unestablished. Supplied-action spectral projectors are not detector records. A field inverse must account for its preparations, complete outcomes, reference state, clock and any charged phase-reference service, and retain invisible or additional modes. The numerical spectral diagnostic does not discharge these execution or physical-identification obligations.

**Closure condition:** Preserve this actual common state, operation and record dictionary when using its occupation proofs, exact infinite sharp response, smooth instrument, finite local modular clock or fixed full-work Gibbs reference. Extend spatial families and attach physical clocks, matter and gravitational observation interfaces with their own controlled errors. Reuse each theorem only after exhibiting its hypotheses on these same objects. Different interactions require new approximation bounds; compiling or rigorously approximating a supplied action does not derive or empirically validate that action.

<a id="finite-contract-coverage"></a>

## Finite-contract coverage and compatible model connections

The complete machine inventory is the `model_supply.contract_coverage` section
of the [premise register](../tracking/premise_register.json). It retains every
canonical claim ID and tracked Lean module, all seven domain coordinates, every
binding's canonical assumptions/imports and every sourced connection map. The
[claim registry](../claims/claim_registry.yaml) owns statements, evidence and
premise roles; the [Lean sources](../Lean/) retain their quantified hypotheses.

Coverage includes non-theorem claims and is a superset of finite contracts.
Lexical declaration counts decide neither finiteness nor proof validity; Lean
builds remain responsible for kernel checks. Registry or source-module additions
require explicit inventory updates. All unbound claims have the same status:
**Open: no same-model instantiation recorded**. This does not invalidate a
theorem on its own fixture or add a physical premise to an explicit non-consumer.

Each binding is a restricted clause, not discharge of its whole registry row.
The JSON retains that row's complete assumption/import union, including inputs
outside the clause. The checker requires exact produced/consumed facts, acyclic
connections and a sourced map for every changed carrier, law, preparation,
record access, control, clock or regime coordinate. These are coverage and
contract checks: mathematical entailment requires review; strings and source
anchors are not proofs. No entry silently discharges a canonical premise.

| Restricted bindings / sourced connections | 77 / 65 |
